Gold supporting: as deletions of the x inactivation center ( xic / xic ) and / or the x inactive specific transcript ( xist / xist ) gene result in failure of cis x - inactivation, mutations in genes from this region might lead to preferential inactivation of one x chromosome or the other.
LLM supporting:  xist ( x - inactive specific transcript ) is the key transcript responsible for x - chromosome inactivation in mammals. it is a long non - coding rna ( lncrna ) that plays a crucial role in the regulation of x chromosome inactivation. the xist gene is part of the xic ( x chromosome inactivation center ) and is involved in the epigenetic modification of the x chromosome, leading to its inactivation. xist expression is typically limited to the inactive x chromosome, while the active x chromosome expresses xist from the other allele, ensuring that only one x chromosome remains active. this reciprocal expression is essential for maintaining the inactive state of the x chromosome and preventing the overexpression of x - linked genes. in cases of skewed x inactivation, one x chromosome may become preferentially inactivated, leading to an imbalance in the expression of x - linked genes, which can have significant physiological consequences, particularly in females with two x chromosomes.

LLM supporting:  supporting sentence : calsequestrin is the main calcium binding protein of the sarcoplasmic reticulum. it is responsible for the storage and release of calcium ions ( ca²⁺ ) in the sarcoplasmic reticulum, which is crucial for muscle contraction and relaxation. calsequestrin binds to ca²⁺ with high affinity, allowing it to sequester and store significant amounts of calcium. when released, these stored calcium ions can be used to trigger the release of additional calcium from the intracellular stores, leading to an increase in intracellular free calcium concentrations that are necessary for various cellular processes, including muscle contraction. mutations in calsequestrin, such as the d307h variant, can impair its function, leading to abnormal calcium signaling and increased susceptibility to arrhythmias. this is because the mutation can reduce the calcium storing capacity of the sarcoplasmic reticulum, leading to disrupted calcium release and uptake mechanisms, and ultimately affecting the regularity of the heartbeat.
